Lemon Tea (27808) prints arginine 500/1,000 mg and guarana caffeine 50/100 mg at one/two scoops, giving total caffeine 200–400 mg/day. Molybdenum (801/802) retains the 75 mg / 100% Daily Value conflict, and 27807 still needs a legible exact-source unit confirmation.

Please carry forward the decisions already supported, add only the missing conclusions and sources, and explicitly retain any unresolved hold. A signature by itself cannot supply missing study facts, but a complete negative or label-limited conclusion can close an assessment.

I want to close the remaining PharmaGuide review in one coordinated batch. We have corrected verified source and label errors, tightened the checks, and preserved every required hold. I need your source-specific decisions on the items below so I can apply the answers together, rebuild once, and verify the complete product set and app.

This is the complete consolidated request for the current frozen review scope. Please return **one marked-up copy of this document**. A completed negative finding, a finding that a study does not apply, or a confirmed label limitation is a valid answer. I am not asking for higher scores, blanket approval, guessed doses, or an exemption for branded ingredients.

**Please do not approve the whole list with a general statement.** For each item, record the actual conclusion and its boundaries. Where information is missing, identify exactly what is needed. That lets us close supported decisions and keep only the specific remaining holds.

## 1. What is included

- **147 ingredient/preparation records**, affecting 6,309 saved record–product links across 5,961 distinct products, plus one generated collagen assessment used by 24 products. Products overlap between records; these numbers must not be added as independent products. Each record is listed in section 6.
- **506 dose/preparation subjects**, covering 4,259 recorded ingredient rows across 3,118 products. These overlap the first list. Every subject is in section 7.
- **171 products with remaining identity or dose limitations in the updated 238-product diagnostic sample**, listed in section 8. This is a sample, not a fresh assessment of the entire catalog. One additional private submission from the saved wider audit is not reproduced here and remains held until its original source is checked.
- The original **15,437-product set is preserved**. No full rebuild or release has been performed for these changes.

The lists are grouped by the underlying ingredient or preparation so the same source question is answered once and applied consistently. Approval for one exact preparation must not automatically spread to every product with a similar name.

## 2. The decision I need for each evidence item

Please choose one conclusion, then fill in the details below:

1. **Support applies within the stated limits.** Identify exactly which preparation, dose, people and outcome are supported.
2. **Reviewed, but support is insufficient, negative or does not apply.** Give the reason and sources; no benefit credit should follow from the rejected or inapplicable source.
3. **The label prevents a fuller assessment.** State precisely what the manufacturer does not disclose. This can be a finished assessment with an honest limitation.
4. **Keep on hold.** State the exact missing paper, correction, preparation specification, label fact or other decision needed. Do not convert unfinished work into “no evidence.”

For **each separately supported source context**, please record:

- Exact ingredient, strain or finished formula, including species, plant part, preparation, standardization and delivery when relevant.
- Source identifier and the specific paper, correction or trial used. Separate independent trials from duplicate reports, reviews and corrections.
- Amount **per day**, units, frequency, duration and whether the amount belongs to the whole formula or an individual ingredient.
- People studied: age, health condition, relevant treatments and other limits.
- Outcome studied, whether it was primary or secondary, and whether results were between groups, within one group, exploratory or post hoc.
- Direction and strength of findings, risk of bias, funding concerns, important null results and uncertainty.
- Whether the finding can apply to the listed preparation and intended use. If not, say why.
- Your name, review date and time with time zone, and an explicit acceptance or rejection of that exact identity–dose–people–outcome relationship.

**Copyable reply for each item**

```text
Item: A___ / B___ / C___ and the printed reference key
Decision: applies within limits / insufficient or negative / does not apply / label limitation / keep held
Exact preparation and delivery:
Sources included, excluded and why:
Daily amount, units, frequency and duration:
People and intended outcome:
Main result and important limitations:
Dose reference decision, if relevant:
Allowed product explanation and any source-backed warning:
Missing information, if any:
Reviewed by / date and time / time zone:
Explicit confirmation or rejection of the above scope:
```

Use a separate source context when the preparation, population or outcome differs. A source rejection and a product mismatch are different findings; please identify which applies. We will retain the original response and enter accepted decisions through the existing review process.

## 3. Rules that must remain intact

- Recognizing an ingredient does not prove its benefit, suitable dose, purity or suitability for a particular person.
- One tested amount is not automatically a minimum effective dose, an optimum, a continuous dose range or a safety upper limit.
- A comparison against another active treatment is not a placebo result. A within-group improvement is not automatically a benefit over placebo.
- A blend trial cannot establish an undisclosed component dose or isolate the benefit of one member. A brand name or patent is not an exemption.
- A single standardized material can be assessed as that material when its amount and identity are disclosed; it should not be mislabeled as an opaque multi-ingredient blend. The actual preparation still needs evidence.
- CFU, enzyme activity, extract mass, whole-herb mass, marker-compound mass and elemental nutrient mass are distinct. Do not invent conversions.
- Unknown testing is not failed testing. Do not assume exact-product purity or third-party verification from a supplier’s general claims.
- Banned/recalled products must remain findable with actionable reasons. No warning should disappear merely to clear an assessment.
- A fully assessed product may score poorly or receive no benefit credit. A calculated high score does not prove the review is complete.

### PrimaVie and other branded materials

Confirm the preparation and source specifications actually supporting a claim. A disclosed single-material amount is not an undisclosed multi-member blend; it still does not establish exact-product purity, general efficacy or every component amount.

### RAW Enzyme275412 and Trace Mineral182990

Exact source structure is repaired. RAW’s620mg enzyme group, separate31mg probiotic group and500millionCFU total are not individual species doses. Trace Mineral’s700mg natural matrix is not700mg elemental minerals; retain separately declared potassium50mg and printed ranges. Confirm only support that actually applies.

### Magnesium biotinate / named combinations

Do not substitute ordinary magnesium, isolated biotin or invented component amounts for an unqualified named preparation. Retain the existing hold unless the exact composition, studied use and source relationship are established.

### Forskolin / chlorella / vitaminC label corrections

241681 literal forskolin25mg is distinct from its250mg parent extract;321393/321394 print cracked-wall chlorella250mg;326244 prints vitaminC180mg as calcium ascorbate with separate calcium40mg. These label corrections do not themselves approve benefit claims.

Other corrected source summaries to verify within the item lists: Mitopure primary peak-power result was null; peak VO2 versus placebo p=.058 and within-group findings must stay distinct. Lutemax must not borrow an EyePromise combination as an independent replication. Sulforaphane failed the stated primary glucose target; subgroup findings remain exploratory. Berberine combination studies and a trial-registration dose discrepancy remain separated. St. John’s wort active-comparator findings must not become placebo claims, and existing interaction warnings stay intact. Rhodiola, ginseng, lemon balm and hyaluronic acid must retain only the outcomes their cited papers actually address.

## 5. Source gaps that need explicit disposition

The two initial source lists contain476 distinct publication identifiers; these are not476independent trials. Among the349 papers in the first evidence worklist,181 have a PMC identifier:157 full-text bodies were retrieved,24 indexed bodies were unavailable, and168 have no PMC identifier. Retrieval is not full review. Publisher access has not been exhausted. An abstract-only check cannot be presented as a full-text assessment.

Please identify the required full texts, inspect relevant corrections/retractions and resolve trial overlap, methods and outcome limitations before accepting a source. Where access remains unavailable, say exactly what cannot be confirmed and keep only that affected conclusion held. Magtein’s correction and PQQ’s correctedTable 4 are known examples. Existing citation flags are not blanket clearance, even where the identifier or title matches.

Two already-inspected corrections also need to remain accurately reflected in any accepted scope:5-MTHF31425504→39083531changes red-cell folate to whole-blood folate in an infant-formula study; it does not establish a general adult benefit. Inositol38163998→39208274adds dataset attribution. These notices must not be counted as independent efficacy trials.

For the15 subjects whose old text overstated an absence of human research—skullcap,noni,corn silk,yarrow,Korean pine,Tremella,Cistanche,French oak,Shatavari,black rice,juniper,peppermint oil,theacrine,kawaratake and Solarplast—we have corrected source existence without assigning a benefit grade or dose reference. Please evaluate the actual preparation and findings. A retracted Shatavari source40434025 must not be used affirmatively; its retraction notice is42820926.

### A001. Affron Saffron Extract — `BRAND_AFFRON`

**Sources:** [PMID 28735826](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28735826/), [PMID 29510352](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29510352/), [PMID 40414301](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40414301/)

**Current statement to verify:** Standardized saffron extract with randomized human evidence for mood and some sleep outcomes in adults and adolescents. Cited studies enrolled 128 adults, 80 youths and 202 adults; no unique aggregate is asserted. The early adult 28 mg dose benefited mood while 22 mg was null; youth reports were inconsistently corroborated by parents. Later secondary outcomes were null, with an exploratory sleep-disturbance subgroup.

### A005. Carnipure L-Carnitine — `BRAND_CARNIPURE`

**Sources:** [PMID 11788381](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11788381/), [PMID 20045157](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20045157/)

**Current statement to verify:** Two small double-blind crossover trials evaluated 2 g/day active L-carnitine delivered as L-tartrate for post-exercise recovery markers (10 resistance-trained men and 18 healthy middle-aged adults). They support a cautious recovery-biomarker signal, not broad energy, performance, cardiovascular, or muscle-growth efficacy. Both cited studies administered 2 g/day of active L-carnitine delivered as L-carnitine L-tartrate, not 2 g of tartrate salt. The 10- and 18-participant crossover studies emphasize recovery biomarkers rather than durable performance outcomes.

Selected questionnaire and wearable outcomes improved, while many measures did not differ from placebo. The original sleep paper reports AIDP funding; the correction content still requires verification. None of these facts supplies reviewer approval or a universal dose threshold.

### A012. Mitopure Urolithin A — `BRAND_MITOPURE`

**Sources:** [PMID 35584623](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35584623/), [PMID 32694802](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32694802/)

**Current statement to verify:** Urolithin A studies distinguish physiological biomarkers from human-study performance. In the four-month ATLAS trial, the prespecified peak-power-output primary endpoint did not differ significantly from placebo. Muscle-strength measures improved, while aerobic-endurance and six-minute-walk improvements at 1,000 mg/day were principally within-group; peak VO2 versus placebo was not significant (p=0.058). Amazentis funded the trial and disclosed employee, board and advisory relationships. The earlier first-in-human study primarily assessed tolerability and mitochondrial biomarkers, not demonstrated durable functional benefit.

**Current statement to verify:** Distilled MSM with explicit randomized human evidence in knee pain and osteoarthritis; newer mild-knee-pain data extend support but remain ingredient-like rather than uniquely product-specific. Studied doses: 6 g/day (PMID 16309928, MSM 3 g twice daily; source not named in the abstract) and 2 g/day (PMID 37447322, ten 200 mg OptiMSM tablets). Generic MSM draws on INGR_MSM, not on this brand record. Cited studies randomized 50 OA and 88 mild-knee-discomfort participants. Selected pain/function findings at 6 g/day and JKOM findings at 2 g/day must remain dose-specific. Exact branded preparation and unique aggregate require methods-level verification. Retained-source scope and limitations: Knee osteoarthritis pain/function in a small pilot (PMID: 16309928) Mild knee-pain JKOM outcomes; not exercise-recovery or measured anti-inflammatory efficacy (PMID: 37447322)

### A023. Sunfiber PHGG — `BRAND_SUNFIBER`

**Sources:** [PMID 26855665](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26855665/), [PMID 31509971](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31509971/)

**Current statement to verify:** Both indexed randomized trials explicitly used Sunfiber PHGG supplied by Taiyo Kagaku; the evidence is for that ingredient preparation, not for a complete Nature's Way retail formula. Niv 2016 found selective improvement in bloating/gas, but not overall IBS severity, abdominal pain, stool frequency or quality of life; 121 randomized and 108 entered the reported intention-to-treat analysis, with substantial differential attrition. Yasukawa 2019 studied healthy adults with loose stools, excluding diagnosed IBS; 44 randomized, 42 stool-efficacy and 40 microbiome analyses. Stool form improved but frequency, GI symptom journals and overall physical/mental quality-of-life summaries did not. Microbiome composition is secondary surrogate evidence; neither indexed trial measured human-study immune benefits or increased SCFA production. The 2016 study received commercial support from Pro Natura and Sunwic AB with declared investigator independence. Taiyo funded and supplied the 2019 study, participated in protocol preparation and employed three authors; these are not independently funded replications. These limitations support the reviewed mixed human-study determination under the existing grading policy.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 2 / 0 / 0. Full review remains open.

**Exact public label editions:** [227960](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/227960.pdf), [228873](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/228873.pdf), [278325](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/278325.pdf), 337859

**Sources:** [PMID 35129040](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35129040/), [PMID 35928723](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35928723/)

**Current statement to verify:** Both trials tested LN18178 (Tesnor), a proprietary blend of standardized Punica granatum fruit-rind and Theobroma cocoa seed extracts, at 200 or 400 mg/day for 56 days in healthy men. Both were run by the ingredient's developer; no independent replication was found. Outcomes included an aging-males' symptom score, hormone levels and hand-grip strength. In the older-men trial, the symptom score was primary and hormone levels and strength were secondary; these short trials do not establish long-term disease outcomes. Pomegranate or cocoa extracts outside this blend cannot borrow this evidence.

**Current statement to verify:** Evidence is for the whole 24-strain plus pomegranate formulation, not individual strains, skin, heart health or micronutrient synthesis. 350 is the baseline sample of the symptom trial, not a sum across studies. No CFU conversion is known or used. PMID 40944126 and 41750436 share protocol NCT04171466; both report 32 randomized, with 27 and 21 completing respectively. The registration reports 46 actual participants across four arms; unique cohort overlap is unresolved, so counts are not summed. Their microbiome/metabolite and barrier outcomes are not independent symptom replication or demonstrated prevention of antibiotic-associated diarrhea. Trial reports establish the named formula, strain list and total potency, not the commercial label's per-blend or individual-strain quantities. Commercial per-blend allocations remain label-identity constraints only.

### A034. Acetyl-L-Carnitine — `INGR_ACETYL_L_CARNITINE`

**Sources:** [PMID 12598816](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12598816/), [PMID 12804452](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12804452/), [PMID 28349514](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28349514/), [PMID 31118753](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31118753/), [PMID 31201734](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31201734/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence is real but mixed and condition-specific. A 2019 Cochrane review found very-low-certainty evidence for diabetic peripheral-neuropathy pain and could not establish safety, while another 2019 review reported a pain reduction across diabetic and HIV-associated neuropathy trials. Cognitive reviews conflict: a positive meta-analysis in mild cognitive impairment or early Alzheimer disease contrasts with a Cochrane dementia review that found no objective cognitive or functional benefit. A separate Cochrane review found insufficient, very-low-quality evidence for cognitive enhancement in healthy adults. This record must not be presented as broad focus, energy, or neuroprotection efficacy.

**Sources:** [PMID 37952511](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37952511/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence indicates immunomodulatory effects, but heterogeneity and methodological variability are substantial. The root (Radix Astragali, huang qi) is the medicinal part named by this family, so a source-declared root preparation or extract is a family-level join; the 2023 meta-analysis pooled heterogeneous human Astragalus interventions and does not establish preparation, route or dose equivalence. Retained-source scope and limitations: Humoral/cellular immune markers with substantial heterogeneity; not retained human-study fatigue efficacy (PMID: 37952511)

### A042. Berberine — `INGR_BERBERINE`

**Sources:** [PMID 18442638](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18442638/), [PMID 27157250](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27157250/)

**Trial registry references (including exclusions/history to distinguish):** [NCT02548832](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02548832), [NCT03281096](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03281096), [NCT04697186](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04697186)

**Current statement to verify:** The cited berberine sources concern different interventions. PMID 18442638 is a small diabetes pilot with a randomized berberine-versus-metformin cohort and a separate add-on cohort; gastrointestinal adverse effects were frequent. PMID 27157250 instead synthesizes a multi-ingredient formula containing red yeast rice, berberine, policosanol, astaxanthin, coenzyme Q10 and folic acid; its effects cannot be assigned to isolated berberine. NCT04697186 concerns berberine plus amoxicillin and rabeprazole for Helicobacter pylori, not standalone supplement treatment. NCT03281096 registered post-colorectal-cancer adenoma prevention, but its brief regimen and arm-level schedule conflict; a universal daily exposure is not established by that registration. Registry phase or completion counts are not human-study efficacy.

**Current statement to verify:** Evidence is limited to complete BCAA mixtures containing leucine, isoleucine, and valine. The most defensible consumer-relevant outcome is a modest reduction in post-exercise muscle soreness and some creatine-kinase measurements. Reviews do not support broad claims of improved athletic performance, superior muscle growth, or body-composition benefit over adequate high-quality protein. The 2024 meta-analysis used 3.15–29.3 g/day; effects tended to be larger above 5 g/day and with longer supplementation.

### A058. Cinnamon Extract — `INGR_CINNAMON_EXTRACT`

**Sources:** [PMID 24019277](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24019277/)

**Current statement to verify:** Meta-analytic human evidence supports cinnamon for modest fasting-glucose and insulin-resistance improvements, but HbA1c and long-term glycemic effects are less consistently robust across trials. Applicability is scoped to Cassia/Cinnulin preparations; Ceylon cinnamon is excluded pending its own source review, which is not a finding of ineffectiveness. Retained-source scope and limitations: Fasting glucose and selected lipid changes in a heterogeneous meta-analysis of 10 RCTs; HbA1c did not significantly change (PMID: 24019277)

### A059. Hydrolyzed Collagen Peptides — `INGR_COLLAGEN_PEPTIDES`

**Sources:** [PMID 37432180](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37432180/), [PMID 37717022](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37717022/), [PMID 39839727](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39839727/), [PMID 40324552](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40324552/)

**Current statement to verify:** Collagen evidence is outcome- and preparation-specific. PMID 37432180 pooled 26 RCTs (1,721 participants) and reported hydration/elasticity benefits with identified study biases. PMID 40324552 pooled 23 RCTs (1,474 participants): overall skin results were favorable, but benefits were absent in non-pharmaceutical-funded and high-quality subgroups, and the authors did not support a current human-study skin-aging treatment claim. Knee-osteoarthritis evidence is separate: PMID 37717022 pooled four trials (507 participants), all rated high risk of bias, with heterogeneous pain benefit and uncertain adverse-event evidence. A specific 10 g hydrolyzed preparation in PMID 39839727 cannot establish universal equivalence or a dose threshold for all collagen products. Generated collagen recovery requires a traceable applicable source context and authentic approval; no human-study clearance is implied.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 3 / 0 / 1. Full review remains open.

### A061. Cranberry Extract — `INGR_CRANBERRY`

**Sources:** [PMID 34473789](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34473789/), [PMID 37947276](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37947276/)

**Current statement to verify:** Family-level evidence concerns cranberry fruit products for prevention of recurrent UTIs in selected populations, not treatment of an existing infection or general immune support. Fruit juice, tablets and powders were studied; equivalence of preparations and PAC doses is uncertain, so this entry carries no dose policy and no universal total-mass threshold is justified. Total concentrate mass is not a PAC dose or a verified human-study minimum. Enrollment 8857 belongs only to the November 2023 Cochrane update (which supersedes the April 2023 version); participants from the overlapping 2021 review are not summed. No efficacy is inferred for cranberry seed oil or leaf extracts.

### A079. Inulin — `INGR_INULIN`

**Sources:** [PMID 34555168](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34555168/), [PMID 35833477](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35833477/), [PMID 38309832](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38309832/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence supports the inulin-type fructan family (inulin, oligofructose and short-chain FOS), not every unspecified prebiotic fiber. PMID 35833477 evaluates chicory-derived preparations: 3–20 g/day, 50 studies/2525 participants, with bowel-function benefits in healthy subjects and no significant bifidogenic benefit in the gastrointestinal-disorder subgroup. Live bibliographic metadata for PMID 35833477 lists BENEO support and author affiliations; precise funder roles require full-text review. PMID 34555168 reviews healthy adults and mixed chain lengths/preparations, with positive and null laxation/calcium findings; support from General Mills is disclosed. Those outcomes cannot be transferred uniformly between inulin and FOS or treated as whole retail-formula trials. PMID 38309832 reports small cardiometabolic risk-factor effects with low or very low certainty, not cardiovascular-event prevention. Review populations overlap and must not be summed as unique enrollment. Historic automated registry discovery supplied 420 enrollment/224 completed trials without an identified NCT receipt; neither is retained as verified evidence metadata. Trial exposure remains a source fact for existing Dose review; no universal studied-dose benchmark is established.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 1 / 0 / 2. Full review remains open.

### A086. L-Carnitine — `INGR_L_CARNITINE`

**Sources:** [PMID 32958033](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32958033/), [PMID 32154768](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32154768/), [PMID 32359762](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32359762/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence exists but is endpoint-, population-, form-, and dose-specific. A seven-RCT meta-analysis reported less post-exercise soreness and short-lived improvement in muscle-damage markers, especially in resistance-training and untrained groups. A 37-RCT meta-analysis reported a modest average body-weight effect, strongest in adults with overweight or obesity and around 2 g/day. A separate systematic review of prolonged use in healthy adults found effects only under specific conditions and raised unresolved long-term TMAO questions. This record is not broad proof of energy, cardiovascular, fat-burning, or athletic-performance efficacy.

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence supports magnesium supplementation for selected outcomes such as blood pressure and migraine prevention, especially in deficiency-risk or higher-dose contexts. ### A093. MSM (Methylsulfonylmethane) — `INGR_MSM`

**Sources:** [PMID 16309928](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16309928/), [PMID 37447322](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37447322/)

**Current statement to verify:** Ingredient-level record for methylsulfonylmethane. Two small randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials: knee osteoarthritis, 50 adults, MSM 3 g twice daily (6 g/day) for 12 weeks, lower WOMAC pain and physical-function impairment, no change in stiffness or total symptoms (PMID 16309928, a pilot); mild knee pain, 88 adults, 2 g/day (OptiMSM) for 12 weeks, primary JKOM total score better than placebo (p = 0.046) (PMID 37447322). These discrete regimens do not establish a general 2 g/day efficacy minimum. Not evidence for energy, recovery or general inflammation claims.

### A100. Phosphatidylserine — `INGR_PHOSPHATIDYLSERINE`

**Sources:** [PMID 20523044](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20523044/), [PMID 21103034](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21103034/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence supports phosphatidylserine as an ingredient, but the cited trials are on soy-derived PS products such as Sharp-PS and Soy-PS rather than on a clearly validated SerinAid-specific trial set. Cited trials randomized 157 to PS-DHA and 78 to soybean-derived PS. Retained-source scope and limitations: Exploratory memory findings from a PS-DHA combination; not isolated-PS stress/exercise recovery evidence (PMID: 20523044) Soy-PS study had overall test-score improvement in all groups, with subgroup/baseline memory findings requiring qualification (PMID: 21103034)

### A109. Saw Palmetto — `INGR_SAW_PALMETTO`

**Sources:** No qualifying source attached; source search and exact preparation still need resolution.

**Trial registry references (including exclusions/history to distinguish):** [NCT00037154](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT00037154), [NCT00603304](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT00603304), [NCT02121613](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02121613)

**Current statement to verify:** Liposterolic extract of Serenoa repens berries (ChEMBL CHEMBL2108769, max_phase=3). This entry holds the GENERIC saw palmetto extract evidence only, and it is null: the CAMUS trial (NCT00603304, n=369, NIDDK/NCCIH Phase 3) found no significant AUA symptom score improvement vs placebo at escalating doses up to 960 mg/day over 72 weeks, and NCT00037154 (n=224, Phase 3) tested 160 mg twice daily in moderate-to-severe BPH. Scope repair 2026-09-18: evidence specific to the hexanic extract Permixon - the PERLES trial (NCT02121613) and the Permixon-only meta-analysis PMID 29694707, whose methods state that 'Articles studying S. repens extracts other than Permixon were excluded' and which reports favourable nocturia and Qmax results - was removed from this entry because it is material-specific and does not transfer to the unspecified or liposterolic extracts covered here. PMID 12006122 was removed for the same reason plus indication: it is an androgenetic-alopecia (hair loss) trial of a botanical 5-alpha-reductase blend of LSESr and beta-sitosterol with 10 actively treated subjects, not saw palmetto monotherapy on this entry's urinary endpoints.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 0 / 0 / 0. Full review remains open.

**Exact public label editions:** [14234](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/14234.pdf), [15715](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/15715.pdf), [178321](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/s3/pdf/178321.pdf), 179983, 18274, 18337, 184926, 185253, 187612, 187614, 188671, 204040, 206963, 210490, 213090, 213096, 218983, 219040, 220281, 228795, 259817, 259832, 26360, 269286, 269589, 270733, 27449, 278359, 287468, 293954, 294541, 294542, 304643, 311538, 312985, 313538, 315813, 315842, 31997, 328054, 328055, 328056, 330651, 330718, 330719, 333944, 33632, 35647, 4138, 46335, 60238, 60240, 62841, 68955, 69696, 71741, 74543, 74635, 74800, 74802, 75045, 75840, 77222, 853, 867, 995, 996

### A112. Spirulina — `INGR_SPIRULINA`

**Sources:** [PMID 12487756](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12487756/), [PMID 25057105](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25057105/), [PMID 30544795](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30544795/), [PMID 34538515](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34538515/), [PMID 35988871](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35988871/), [PMID 37263369](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37263369/), [PMID 38401078](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38401078/)

**Trial registry references (including exclusions/history to distinguish):** [NCT01752972](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01752972), [NCT04988347](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04988347), [NCT05016557](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT05016557)

**Current statement to verify:** Reviewed mixed human-study findings with preparation- and population-specific applicability. PMID 34538515 separates seven human human-study studies from 27 preclinical studies and reports null LDL-C/HbA1c findings alongside positive metabolic biomarkers. PMID 35988871 reports eight direct Spirulina-placebo studies/420 participants, 6–16 weeks, with a small LDL-C reduction and VERY LOW certainty. Its 131-trial/13062-participant total spans eleven nutraceuticals and is not a Spirulina study count. Later lipid review PMID 37263369 supports a lipid biomarker signal, not longevity or cardiovascular-event prevention; overlapping reviews must not be summed as independent trials. The HIV trial reports an antioxidant surrogate without between-group immunological/virological benefit; the pediatric nephrotic-syndrome study reports adjunctive within-group changes. No immune protection, longevity or ordinary-dose muscle-recovery claim is established. The existing conservative rct_single/tier_2 scoring representation is retained; source summaries do not automatically upgrade numerical study-type credit. The HIV and pediatric adjunctive studies are not evidence of general longevity benefit.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 2 / 0 / 5. Full review remains open.

### A117. Vitamin B3 (Niacin) — `INGR_VITAMIN_B3_NIACIN`

**Sources:** [PMID 11498460](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11498460/), [PMID 15642562](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15642562/), [PMID 25141367](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25141367/), [PMID 9732910](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9732910/)

**Trial registry references (including exclusions/history to distinguish):** [NCT00619541](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT00619541), [NCT01411436](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT01411436), [NCT02961829](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02961829)

**Current statement to verify:** Niacin has strong lipid-modifying evidence at pharmacologic doses (1-3g/day). AIM-HIGH and HPS2-THRIVE found no incremental benefit when added to statin therapy, but standalone evidence from the Coronary Drug Project remains positive. Supplement doses (50-500mg) have less direct evidence. The cited combination, uncontrolled and selected disease-population studies require separate applicability review. Nicotinamide in HIV research is not equivalent to nicotinic-acid lipid efficacy.

### A118. Vitamin B6 — `INGR_VITAMIN_B6`

**Sources:** [PMID 36719452](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36719452/), [PMID 10334745](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10334745/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence for vitamin B6 is indication-specific. The clearest supplementation support is for nausea and vomiting of pregnancy and for some premenstrual symptoms, while broader neurological or cardiovascular claims are usually tied to deficiency correction or combination B-vitamin regimens. NIH ODS also emphasizes neuropathy risk at high chronic doses. Retained-source scope and limitations: Nausea/vomiting during pregnancy, with standalone and combination interventions kept distinct (PMID: 36719452) Premenstrual symptoms in generally low-quality trials; not retained homocysteine efficacy evidence (PMID: 10334745)

### A120. Vitamin D3 — `INGR_VITAMIN_D3`

**Sources:** [PMID 28202713](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28202713/), [PMID 30293909](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30293909/)

**Current statement to verify:** Scope is supplementation outcomes only. PMID 28202713 supports reduced acute respiratory infection risk, clearest in people deficient at baseline. Mood and bone claims were removed because this record does not support them: no mood trial is cited at all, and its musculoskeletal reference (PMID 30293909) found no fracture or fall prevention and no clinically meaningful BMD gain from supplementation. Vitamin D's physiological role in calcium absorption is well established but is not a supplementation outcome and needs its own supporting trial before a bone goal returns here; INGR_CALCIUM and other records still carry Joint & Bone Health. 2024 Endocrine Society guidelines recommend empiric supplementation for specific populations. OWNERSHIP: this file owns INTERVENTION evidence (what a trial showed). Product-facing goal_matches are owned by the bone_health synergy cluster in synergy_cluster.json, which carries the NUTRIENT-FUNCTION relationship (vitamin D aids calcium absorption, NIH ODS) and is unaffected by this record. Do not restore a bone goal here to 'fix' a goal_matches question.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 1 / 0 / 1. Full review remains open.

### A121. Protein Supplementation — `INGR_WHEY_PROTEIN`

**Sources:** [PMID 28698222](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28698222/), [PMID 39303495](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39303495/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human systematic reviews support dietary protein supplementation for modest additional gains in lean mass, muscle size, and strength when paired with sustained resistance training. The reviewed sources include whey, casein, milk, soy, pea, and studied source blends. A generic Protein macro declaration or an unspecified protein powder/blend does not identify one of these sources. Source quality differences remain a Formulation question, and the evidence does not imply further benefit once total daily protein intake is already adequate.

### A122. White Kidney Bean Extract — `INGR_WHITE_KIDNEY_BEAN`

**Sources:** [PMID 42066439](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42066439/), [PMID 39170208](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39170208/)

**Current statement to verify:** Oral white kidney bean (Phaseolus vulgaris) extract is rich in alpha-amylase inhibitors. A 2026 ingredient-level meta-analysis of 8 randomized controlled trials (543 adults with overweight or obesity) found reductions in weight, BMI, fat mass, waist and hip circumference, with no significant change in fasting glucose, triglycerides or insulin and no serious adverse events. The pooled weight effect is modest (-1.62 kg). IMPORTANT LIMITATION carried deliberately: generic extract milligrams without verified inhibitory activity do not guarantee equivalence to standardized study preparations, and no potency conversion is proposed or implied. Evidence remains independent of disclosed amount. The former 1,000 mg/day eligibility floor is retired: the verified meta-analysis abstract supplies no universal daily exposure boundary. Phaseolean 1,500/3,000 mg/day regimens are preparation-specific; generic extract milligrams cannot establish comparable potency. Dose retains reference uncertainty until exact comparable preparation identity and standardization are verified.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 1 / 0 / 1. Full review remains open.

### A123. Zinc acetate/gluconate lozenges — `INGR_ZINC_PICOLINATE`

**Current statement to verify:** This record concerns early, short-term treatment of acute common colds in adults using zinc acetate or gluconate lozenges; it does not establish prevention or efficacy of other zinc forms or swallowed capsules. Evidence applicability is independent of quantity. Dose describes the separate 80-92 and 192-207 mg/day research clusters, not a continuous efficacy range or an intake recommendation. Nutrient adequacy, Safety and upper-limit assessment remain independent; trial correspondence does not establish routine-safe intake.

### A128. Boswellia Serrata — `PRECLIN_BOSWELLIA`

**Sources:** [PMID 32680575](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32680575/), [PMID 35512759](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35512759/), [PMID 39092235](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39092235/)

**Current statement to verify:** Human evidence concerns Boswellia serrata extracts for osteoarthritis pain, stiffness and function, not stress, longevity or broad immune benefit. This family-level association does not establish that an unspecified resin extract equals Aflapin or Boswellin Super in standardized composition, dose or onset. Enrollment 545 is the seven-trial 2020 review only; participants from the newer trials are not summed. No generic extract-mass threshold is established here. The former UNII X7B7P649WQ identifies BOSWELLIA SERRATA WHOLE, not an extract, and is not used as an extract identifier. Real-label review (2026-09-05) added the printed extract spellings gum extract, standardized extract, Apres-Flex, Casperome and frankincense phytosome as family-level extract preparations; a bare Boswellia serrata row with only a boswellic-acid form stays unmatched until its source states an extract. The primary outcome uses the locked vocabulary label Joint & Bone Health for osteoarthritis pain, stiffness and function.

**Source access — bodies retrieved / indexed unavailable / no PMC:** 2 / 0 / 1. Full review remains open.

Retained-source scope and limitations: No advantage over placebo in a 47-person DGL duodenal-ulcer trial (PMID: 4933135) Preparation-specific GutGard dyspepsia symptoms, H. pylori test outcomes and reflux symptoms; not proof of generic ulcer healing (PMID: 21747893; 23606875; 39929150)

### A141. Sulforaphane — `PRECLIN_SULFORAPHANE`

**Current statement to verify:** Sulforaphane studies concern specific broccoli-sprout preparations and indication-specific outcomes. The prediabetes trial PMID 39929977 did not meet its prespecified primary target of a 0.3 mmol/L fasting-glucose reduction versus placebo. The observed overall reduction was 0.2 mmol/L (95% CI -0.44 to -0.01; p=0.04); responder and microbiome subgroup analyses were exploratory and require confirmation. Gastrointestinal adverse effects occurred. The former-smoker trial measures bronchial Ki-67, a biomarker, not proven cancer prevention; autism evidence remains preparation- and population-specific.

## 7. Complete dose/preparation list — 506 subjects

For eachBitem, choose one:

- **Supported reference:** specify exact preparation, purpose, people, tested daily regimen, units, source and what the numbers actually mean.
- **Completed without a general numerical reference:** give a dated, source-backed reason and any useful studied regimen; do not turn the absence of a universal curve into a claim of no benefit or personal suitability.
- **Genuine label limitation:** identify the undisclosed or conflicting fact; no invented amount or conversion.
- **Still held:** specify the exact source or preparation decision needed.

The five draft no-general-curve conclusions for aloe vera, flaxseed, green tea leaf, lignans and pygeum are **not approved or active**. Please evaluate each, rather than accepting them as a group. A lack of a dose reference does not automatically require the same type of approval as a positive benefit claim.

The following table is the complete subject list. “No attached source” means source work remains; it does not mean no research exists. Existing preparation wording is included for verification and may itself be incomplete or wrong. All 506 remain open.

## 8. Remaining identity and dose questions in the diagnostic sample

This list uses the final bounded replay for the original238 products.171 still have an identity or dose limitation (130 identity,51 dose,10 overlapping). These are not171 independent literature reviews, and they are not a fresh full-catalog count. Some items should close as a documented manufacturer limitation or an intentionally unassessable product; do not force a score.

**What I need:** confirm the exact identity/preparation when source evidence supports it; reject wrong transfers; otherwise state the specific missing identity, form, unit, constituent amount or label correction. A question about how our software maps an already-confirmed identity stays with me to fix. You are not being asked to debug software or approve an invented form-quality grade.

**Important source facts:** labels801/802/31063actually print molybdenum75mg with100%DV;27807has the same saved conflict but its PDF is not independently legible enough.231334/231335/263865print vitaminA6000mcgDFE.223563/223572print vitaminA900RAEmg and vitaminD100mg/50mg. Do not silently reinterpret these as other units.315691declares potassium iodide130mg as compound mass for emergency use; its intentionally unassessable disposition is already documented, and it is not a request for a routine-dose endorsement.260262does not establish individual EPA/DHA from its total omega-3 declaration.306350does not separately declare collagen mass from protein10g.268493’s9gmaterial includes coconut andacacia; it is not proof of9gpureMCT or a constituent amount. Corn Silk312640/312713/330886has restored identity but no invented powder-versus-extract grade.

The separately saved private-submission case remains held and is not included in this forwardable public-label list. Its original submitted label must be checked before it can close; no approval of this document should be interpreted as approving an unseen submission.

## 9. Items deliberately outside the affirmative approval queue

Five records retain sources for reference but cannot supply positive benefit credit: `INGR_MOLYBDENUM`, `INGR_PROPOLIS`, `INGR_SLIPPERY_ELM`, `INGR_VITAMIN_E` and `PRECLIN_BETAINE_HCL`. Three records are historical-only in this saved contributor audit: `INGR_BIFIDOBACTERIUM_BIFIDUM`, `INGR_GLYCINE` and `INGR_SAME`. Unused history does not block publication. This is not permission to delete sources or to reuse an old approval for new intake. If any becomes an actual contributing source later, its required review must be satisfied before use.

## 10. Existing citation flags — 146 rows requiring explicit disposition, not blanket reapproval

The wider source audit also retains146 automated mismatch flags. These are **flags, not146 newly proven scientific errors**. Some references are already suppressed or retained only as history; some use DOI/PMC identifiers rather than a PMID and need a technical check. A title-word mismatch alone cannot decide whether a source supports a claim.

I will trace which references actually contribute to a current assessment or warning and handle format-only/history-only issues. An unused or already-excluded historical reference does not block publication by itself. For any source that is actually relied on, the decision must be explicit: it supports the exact recorded claim within stated limits, it does not support that claim and stays excluded, or a named replacement/source fact is required. Please do not reactivate an excluded source or remove a real warning simply to clear a flag.

The complete flagged list is below so these references do not become a hidden follow-up request. An example is the oral-contraceptive/magnesium record: its inherited unrelated PMID is already marked for revision and the warning is suppressed; that source must not silently become evidence again. The table identifies the current reference to check, not a claim that its title alone establishes a defect.

## 11. What will count as closed

For every A/B/C item and any actually relied-on D reference, I need an explicit decision, supporting sources, exact scope, your name/date/time zone and any remaining source gap. A repeated preparation may share one answer if every covered item is named and the boundaries match. “Approved generally,” a checked citation title or a positive score is not enough.

Once your consolidated reply is returned, I will:

1. Apply accepted findings through the existing ingredient, dose and evidence records; preserve the original reply and the reasons for exclusions.
2. Fix any remaining software mapping issues against those decisions and run the corresponding regression checks.
3. Reconcile every listed item as accepted within scope, completed negative/inapplicable, documented label limitation, intentionally unassessable, or still held for a specifically named missing fact.
4. Rebuild once from the first required stage when the required source decisions are complete.
5. Verify the entire rebuilt product set and app, including source explanations, dose limitations, safety warnings and the original 20 examples.
6. Keep publication separate until those checks pass and I authorize it.

A returned review closes a supported scientific decision; I will only mark the product issue fully closed after the rebuilt output shows the correct result. We will not reopen a settled item because of a different subjective score, a wording preference or another request to “review again.” Reopening requires a concrete new source, changed label/preparation, correction/retraction, safety finding or reproducible defect. Any item still missing information stays clearly held with one precise next action rather than restarting the whole review.

The original 20 checks are label editions270330,310116,261951,13801,185032,299952,317006,19067,12129,12153,1059,213846,201101,329943,328014,213826,19598,271115,1179and232839. Their scores are not targets to reproduce; their identities, source applicability, amounts, limitations and explanations are the checks.

Please return one consolidated response covering these lists, including a clear list of any items you cannot finish and exactly what would resolve each. I want this to be one bounded closeout, with real uncertainty preserved and no unsupported claims.

Thank you,
Sean
